Rejected — `frames_ok` 764, `Hello` 29, `hello_session_ids` still `[4]`, 99 never reaching the model. **Measured cost of one lost HELLO: 21 live frames deferred (promoted 631→610, blocked 14→35, total unchanged at 645) = 0.98 s against a 1.0 s re-announce period, then self-healing** — TODO-56 quantified rather than argued. A 1 689-byte LIVE_FRAME corruption lost exactly one frame with no collateral. **Bound: this proves the RECEIVER rejects; no corrupted byte was transmitted, and the Teensy/UART/physical layer are untouched.**
> 5. **Unknown packet-type tolerance — ✅ PROVEN OFFLINE 2026-09-26.** Well-formed `0xFE` (valid sync, length and **CRC**) injected at a verified frame boundary, byte 61 007. `frames_ok` 766, **`crc_failures` 0**, every known packet count and the entire router status identical to clean. Zero-length `0xFD` also handled. **The parser tolerates it while the report flags it** — both correct, since an unknown type from our own firmware means version skew. Same receiver-only bound as above.
> 6. **Reconnect / reboot / session-change matrix — PARTIALLY ADVANCED 2026-09-27.** The session counter incrementing across real power cycles is now **`OBS`, not `INFER`**: three boots appear in preserved artifacts — **4** (2026-09-26 ×3 captures), **6** (2026-09-27 ×2 captures, same id in both), **7** (the RESULT run). Session **5** was never captured, so **a receiver must treat any change as a new session and must not expect +1.** **Still open:** no reboot has been observed *during* a live capture, and nothing has tested a running receiver recovering without being restarted. Runbook step 10 is untouched.
> 7. **Pi→Teensy direction** — physically disconnected by design.
>
> **🛑 Integration finding — `/dev/serial0` is NOT the header UART on this Pi 5.** `serial0 → ttyAMA10` received **nothing**; the 40-pin header UART is **`/dev/ttyAMA0`**. `MEAS`: continuity passed both conductors, and a **RIGOL DHO804 showed a 0–3.3 V UART waveform at Teensy pin 1 and at Pi physical pin 10** — bytes were arriving while Linux read a different peripheral. **When a capture is empty, check the device before the wire.** Recorded as a `[STOP]` in the runbook and in `capture.sh`'s zero-byte path.
> **🔧 Tooling defect fixed — `capture.sh` reported a failure it never performed.** `stty … min 0 time 1` let a no-data read return immediately, so a 30 s request exited after ~1 s reporting 0 bytes. Correctly refused as evidence. Now `min 1 time 0`, plus an elapsed-vs-requested check that exits non-zero when short. Five regression tests guard both defects; a negative control confirms each fails when reverted.

**🟢 Closed in source (protocol v2, `PI_PROTOCOL_VERSION 2`):** all five 🔴 findings plus six 🟡.
- **L1 — clamp state now on the wire.** Per transmitted bin, one byte carries the number of source bins CLAMPED (high nibble) and VALID (low nibble), plus frame-level `tf_clamped_count` over all 512 source bins. The rendering contract is written into `data_sender.h`: `clamped == ratio` means draw a saturation indicator, **not** the rail value; `valid == 0` means pen-lift.
- **L2 — decimation domains separated.** Linear spectra decimate by **maximum** (peak-preserving: an arithmetic mean under-reported a lone peak by 12 dB); `tf_db` keeps the dB mean. Both choices are now stated on the wire contract rather than being undocumented defaults.
- **L3 — NaN coverage preserved** via the valid nibble; a bin with no energised source bins emits NaN.
- **R1 `result_valid`, R2 `calibration_provenance[96]`, R3 `calibration_type` + 5 warning bits, R5 structured `firmware_version`, R6 `fft_size`/capture blocks/node channels/`line_in_level`** — all transmitted.
- **T2 → structured HELLO packet** (`0x06`) with numeric protocol/bin/baud fields and a capability bitmap. **T4** closed by that bitmap: the Pi can now distinguish "monitor mode off" from "monitor mode not compiled in", so it will not render a control that cannot respond. `MODE_PRECISION`/`MODE_LF` bits stay clear because `arm()` still rejects them — advertising them would be a lie the Pi would act on.
- **T3 → VIEW packet** (`0x07`). `presentation_restoreLive()` no longer sends a magic `"VIEW:LIVE"` status string.
- **T5** gone with the rewritten code path; `_PI` and `_NONE` now compile with **zero warnings**.
- **R4 / R7 deliberately deferred** (result auto-spectra = +4 KB/packet, revisit after T1; `phase_unwrapped`/`tf_raw_mag_db` recoverable or redundant). Recorded as decisions, not omissions.

**🟡 T1 — mitigated architecturally, NOT YET MEASURED. This is the only thing standing between v2 and a freeze.**
The rule is now explicit in source: **presentation transport must be lossy before measurement becomes lossy.** LIVE_FRAME is best-effort/droppable/latest-wins; RESULT is reliable, staged and drained across loop iterations by `dataSender_service()`; events are best-effort. Every write is gated on `availableForWrite()` and **no blocking write remains**. The TX buffer is extended to 4 KB via `addMemoryForWrite()` — a `static_assert` enforces that it exceeds one framed live frame, because otherwise `availableForWrite()` could never report room and *every* frame would silently drop.
Safety net: `measurement_state` rides in every live frame, so a dropped ABORTED costs the reason string, not the fact.
**Note the trade made knowingly:** v2's live frame is *larger* than v1's (1 689 B vs 1 559 B, ~36.7 ms vs ~33.8 ms predicted) because closing L1/L3 costs 130 bytes of coverage data. Those bytes are what stop the Pi rendering a rail as a measurement, and the larger frame is only safe *because* it is now droppable.
**Proof required (Phase 10 step 11/12, TODO-35):** measured max call duration in **microseconds**; block timing with a Pi consuming indistinguishable from `DISPLAY_TARGET_NONE`; and block timing unchanged with the Pi **absent, stalled, and yanked mid-result**. A high drop count with a low max call time is the policy working. **Do not close T1 on a source reading.**

**🔺 THE MITIGATION CLAUSE BELOW IS CORRECTED 2026-09-26 (TODO-57).** Where this item said a dropped ABORTED was safe because *"measurement_state rides in every live frame"*, that was **false**: `presentation_updateLive()` is gated to `IDLE|ARMED`, so during ABORTED no live frame is sent at all. The actual safety net is the periodic `DS_PACKET_STATE` (0x08) snapshot, which repairs both the state and the machine-readable abort reason within 500 ms. **The T1 report gained a `State:` line with `state_sent`/`state_dropped`**, so all four runs must be captured against this build.

**Instrumentation shipped for that measurement:** `dataSender_getStats()` / `dataSender_printDiagnostics()`, surfaced as the `u` serial command and printed inside the existing 5 s diagnostic window so transport figures sit directly beneath block timing — the question is never "how many frames dropped" alone, it is "did transmission perturb acquisition".

**📌 `results_sent` IS NOT DELIVERY EVIDENCE (clarified 2026-09-26, TODO-59).** It counts bytes accepted into the UART, not results received. The protocol has no return path and no acknowledgement, so the Teensy cannot know what arrived. **Do not close T1, or any Phase 10 step, on a Teensy-side result count** — before TODO-59 a RESULT could be corrupted on the wire by an interleaved packet and still be counted as sent. Result delivery is proven at the Pi or not at all.

**🔺 THE T1 REPORT CHANGED 2026-09-26 (TODO-56) — capture all four runs against the NEW build.** `dataSender_service()` now re-announces HELLO at 1 Hz whenever no RESULT is staged, and the report carries a new line: session id plus `hello_sent` / `hello_dropped`. Two consequences for the acceptance matrix, neither optional:
- **HELLO drops are counted separately and are EXPECTED in run 3.** A suspended reader keeps the UART full by design, so roughly one HELLO drop per second of the window is the policy working — it is *not* a defect and *not* a reason to fail the run. They were deliberately kept out of `events_dropped` precisely so that figure stays readable.
- **The re-announce is a new periodic call on the presentation path**, so it contributes to `send_calls` and can contribute to `send_us_max`. It is ~1.02 ms of *wire* time and microseconds of *call* time, but **that is a prediction, not a measurement** — it is exactly what T1 exists to check. **Do not reuse any earlier report as a control.** Run 1 (`_NONE`) is unaffected: `data_sender.cpp` is compiled out entirely for that target.

**Protocol acceptance tests** (docs/pi_link_protocol_audit.md): A the truth question per field · B no new blocking write · C layout drift cannot be silent (`static_assert` + a runtime check that refuses to send a drifted packet) · D receiver survives unknown packet types · E renderer cannot perturb measurement.

**Original audit findings, retained below for the reasoning trail:**
**Why:** `data_sender`'s protocol was written before the clamp-marker work, the
confidence-denominator fix and the insight/provenance model reached their current
form, and had never been checked against them. Promoting it to production makes it
a compatibility surface between two codebases.
**Findings:** [docs/pi_link_protocol_audit.md](../docs/pi_link_protocol_audit.md) —
**17 findings, five 🔴, four of them failures of the ADR-001 invariant** ("the
Teensy determines truth"). Every invariant failure is the same mistake: the
firmware computes a verdict and does not put it on the wire.
- **L1 🔴 the TF clamp mask is not transmitted.** `tf_clamped_bits[]` /
  `tf_clamped_count` exist precisely so consumers can distinguish a saturation
  rail from a measurement, and `audio_engine.h:310` makes checking them mandatory
  for any consumer that renders `tf_db` to a human. `sendLiveFrame()` sends
  neither. **This re-opens TODO-29 on a new surface** — the Pi would plot a flat,
  confident, entirely fictional −24.0 dB curve exactly as the serial diagnostic
  did in 33 of 33 windows on 2026-09-07 before `aa9a795` fixed it.
- **R1 🔴 `result_valid` is not transmitted at all.** The Pi must infer validity
  from the quality enum — re-deriving a judgement the Teensy already made.
- **R2/R3 🔴 `calibration_provenance`, `calibration_type` and
  `calibration_warnings` are not transmitted.** The result view is specified to
  show provenance (README, TODO-12 proof criterion) and cannot. A calibrated
  result would display with its own warnings silently dropped.
- **T1 🔴 blocking UART writes sit in the real-time super-loop.** A 1 559-byte
  live frame is ~33.8 ms of wire time at 460 800 baud — **73 % of the measured
  46.3 ms audio block period** — and `DISPLAY_UPDATE_EVERY_N_FRAMES` is 1, so one
  is sent every block. A max-size RESULT is ~214 ms, ~4.6 block periods. Same
  failure shape as the RA8875 `waitPoll()` incident, different peripheral.
  **⚠️ This is an analytic prediction from baud rate and packet size, NOT a bench
  measurement** — the TX buffer size and whether Audio-library DMA survives a
  stall have not been measured. Instrument it before recording it as a finding.
- **T2 🟡 the protocol version is a human-readable string** the Pi must parse to
  establish its own compatibility contract.
**What:** close L1, R1, R2, R3 and T2 — they change the payload shape — then
version and freeze the format. Measure T1 before choosing its fix.
**Proof:** a frozen, numbered protocol spec; a Teensy-side packet dump showing
clamp state, `result_valid` and provenance present; and a measured (not predicted)
worst-case loop-blocking figure for both packet types.

> **✅ 2026-09-27 — a Teensy-authored RESULT reached the Pi intact, and the negative control for it exists too.** Two records, deliberately separate: [`2026-09-27-phase10-result-transport.md`](evidence/2026-09-27-phase10-result-transport.md) (real 1 kHz stimulus → RESULT) and [`2026-09-27-phase10-armed-timeout-negative-control.md`](evidence/2026-09-27-phase10-armed-timeout-negative-control.md) (**no** stimulus → `ARMED` → `ABORTED("armed timeout")` → sticky `TIMEOUT`). **The negative control was originally described as a failed stimulated measurement and that description is withdrawn** — no stimulus was playing after midnight on 2026-09-27, and peak source −63.9 dBFS against peak return −65.7 dBFS is the signature of an unplugged input, not of a measurement that went wrong. A timeout with no input is the arm threshold working.

Artifact kept on the Pi, not committed.

The earlier 0 B was the RESULT drain overflowing the 4 096 B ring, **not** live frames. Promoted from candidate to established (TODO-63).
> **🛑 RUNS 3 AND 4 REMAIN UNEXECUTED and their original premise is void.** `Serial1` has **no hardware flow control** — no `attachCts`/`attachRts` anywhere, RTS/CTS not wired, only TX and GND. Stopping the Pi's userspace reader starves the *Pi*; the Pi's kernel tty buffer discards while the Teensy keeps transmitting at baud rate. **Run 3 cannot produce Teensy-side backpressure, and a stalled reader cannot extend the ~102 ms RESULT drain for run 4.** Live proof: with nothing reading `/dev/ttyAMA0`, the Teensy reported **160 182 live frames sent, 0 dropped**. Both runs need redesigning around what this wiring can actually prove.
> **🔧 T1 PREPARED 2026-09-27 — no flash, no bench action, nothing run.** [`docs/T1_run_sheet.md`](../docs/T1_run_sheet.md) carries the four runs as a fill-in sheet plus [`pi/tools/t1_diff.py`](../pi/tools/t1_diff.py), a mechanical differ for the four `T` reports. **The sheet defines no acceptance criteria** — step 11 and this item remain the authority, and the differ deliberately **refuses to judge** `block_ms_*` against the control because no authority here defines "indistinguishable" and inventing a threshold would manufacture a criterion. It enforces only what is stated absolutely (`blocks_skipped`/`skip_events`/`largest_block_gap` = 0 in every run) plus experiment-control checks.
> **★ Minimum flash sequence: ONE flash completes all four runs; a second restores the known-good image.** Runs 2–4 are all `_PI` and the board already runs `26f2bee`, so they go **first** at zero flash cost — and if run 2 fails, the known-good image was never disturbed. Then one flash to `_NONE` for run 1 (the control), then the **archived `26f2bee-PI` hex** back (never a rebuild — `__DATE__`/`__TIME__` make builds non-reproducible, so a rebuild would break the flashed-artifact match). Run 1's position is an *analysis* dependency, not an execution one; drift is controlled by logging SOURCE peak per run and by an optional run-2-repeat after the restore flash.
> **✅ `_NONE` boot/electrical review done from source: no routing, relay, generator, injection, GPIO or analog change.** `guitar_rig_analyzer.ino` has **no `#if DISPLAY_TARGET` guard anywhere** — every hardware init call is unconditional, so relays stay LOW/de-energised, return stays TS, injection stays false, generator amplitude stays 0, and all four jack pins stay `INPUT_PULLUP`. Exactly two differences, both benign: `Serial1.begin()` never runs so **Teensy pin 1 stays high-Z** (the Pi will receive nothing in run 1 — *not a fault*), and the **EEPROM session-counter write does not happen**, which makes `_NONE` strictly one side effect safer than the image already on the board.
> **🛑 THREE DECISIONS ARE OWED BEFORE ANY RUN, all recorded in the sheet rather than resolved:** **(D1)** step 11 and this item both require the **validation suite `1`–`4` to run in run 3**, which collides with the standing "do not casually invoke Phase 9" rule — three options offered, including deferring the clause and substituting a USB-liveness check. **(D2)** run 4 asks for a wire pull inside the **measured 102 ms** RESULT drain, which is **not hand-achievable**; recommended resolution is to perform run 4 with the reader already suspended so the drain stalls for seconds, with `result_bytes_abandoned > 0` as the objective confirmation the run happened. **(D3)** **do not break the link at Pi physical pin 10** — it is adjacent to **pin 8 (GPIO14/TXD0), a driven output**, and a jumper slipping across would put two push-pull drivers against each other; break it mid-cable or at Teensy pin 1, whose neighbour is a harmless loopback.
> **✅ D1/D2/D3 DECIDED BY THE OPERATOR 2026-09-27, and one of them permanently limits this item.** **D1: validation Tests 1–4 are NOT run during T1** — the standing Phase 9 rule is not relaxed for a timing experiment. **The step 11 clause *"the validation suite (`1`–`4`) still runs and passes with the Pi stalled"* is therefore recorded as UNEXECUTED/DEFERRED, NOT substituted.** A USB-liveness observation may be collected as supplemental evidence and **must never be written up as satisfying that clause. Consequence: even a clean four-run matrix CANNOT fully close T1 or this item** — the correct resulting status is *"T1 acquisition acceptance satisfied; step 11 validation-suite clause unexecuted."* **D2:** run 4 uses a deliberately stalled reader rather than a manual pull inside the ~102 ms normal drain — approved in principle, **gated on verifying from current source what actually increments `result_bytes_abandoned`** (not inferred from the counter's name). **D3:** no disconnect at Pi physical pin 10; exact break point to be specified and reviewed before run 4. **No physical disconnect is authorised.**
> **🔧 RUN 2 PREPARED AS A DETERMINISTIC OPERATOR CARD 2026-09-27, NOT EXECUTED:** [`docs/T1_run2_procedure.md`](../docs/T1_run2_procedure.md). Window **fixed at exactly 120 s for all four runs** (authority requires "≥60 s, identical"; 120 s removes the judgment). Run 2 uses the **repo tool `capture.sh`**, not an ad-hoc reader — run 2 needs no suspension, and `capture.sh` cannot be used for runs 3/4 because its `timeout` leaves a **pending SIGTERM on a `kill -STOP`ped process**. `t`/`T` semantics verified in source: **separate case labels** (unlike `u`/`U`, `i`/`I`, `x`/`X`), handler reads **one character and flushes the line**, so **every stray keystroke in that window is a command — `v` *and* `V` both run the full validation suite**, which D1 forbids.
> **✅ METRIC-SCOPE AUDIT (item 12, done call-site by call-site):** everything in the `[T1]` block is window-scoped and reset by `t`; `AudioMemoryUsageMaxReset()` is called from exactly one place (inside `timingProbe_reset()`); `timingProbe_reset()` from exactly two (`setup()` and `t`); `dataSender_resetStats()` from exactly one (`dataSender_init()`). No other command (`r`, `u`, `m`, `x`, `j`, `k`) touches any of them. The sketch's own `s_total_blocks`/`s_total_displays`/`s_loop_iterations` are boot-scoped but **never reach the report**. The skip detector is safe across a mid-stream `t` because `s_have_block_number` is cleared, so a fresh baseline is established rather than a false gap. **`[Link]` remains the sole boot-scoped block, and even with one boot per run it covers slightly MORE than the window** (settle time + boot self-test) — write transport figures up as *per boot*, never *per window*.
> **⚠️ NEW READING CAVEAT — `loop_us_max` is dominated by the 5 s diagnostic print, not by the transport.** [`.ino:930`](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L930) deliberately closes the loop-body timer as the **last statement in `loop()`**, so the print is inside the measured body. That print is **one line under `_NONE` and ~14 under `_PI`**, which makes `loop_us_max` the single compared metric most contaminated by the print asymmetry. Step 11 asks only that it be *"comparable"*; the decisive metrics are `block_ms_*` and `blocks_skipped`. **Do not read an inflated `loop_us_max` in runs 2–4 as a transport fault.**
> **📌 Sequencing note.** The receive path is now proven, so the renderer is no longer blocked on *transport existence* — but TODO-36 blocks it on **T1**, and that rule stands: a renderer embeds frame-rate and latency assumptions T1 could invalidate, and at **79 % link occupancy** that is not a theoretical concern.
> **✅ Producer side done and proven on hardware.** The board runs `26f2bee` (`Build: Sep 26 2026 16:36:53 v0.6.0`, sha256 `24b71d73…`), emitting all four producers at measured cadences: LIVE_FRAME **21.6/s**, STATE 2/s, HELLO 1/s, ROUTER_STATUS 1/s ≈ **25.6 packets/s, ~36.5 KB/s, 79 % of the 460800 wire**. Transport call time max **282 µs**; block period 46.3–46.7 ms against 46.4 expected. Journal 2026-09-26 (8).
> **✅ Consumer side written and tested offline.** Framing, parser, receiver state model, and `pi/tools/{capture.sh,replay.py}` — 173 tests. `capture.sh` writes raw bytes only; `replay.py` reports packets by type, rates, CRC failures, resync loss, unknown types, session id, final state and all four detector states. **Its verdict is transport-only: analog content can never fail a replay.**
> **🛑 BLOCKED ON PHYSICAL PI ACCESS — and this is the last real gap.** Nothing has *received* a byte. The parser and the firmware still share one author's reading of `data_sender.h`, so **the shared-author evidence loop is unbroken.** One 30 s capture replayed through `replay.py` breaks it.
> **Next bench session, in order:** runbook **2a** on the Pi (five commands, all answers recorded — the UART device, console ownership, pin function, HAT contest) → receive-only wiring, **Teensy pin 0 stays unconnected** → power cycle **with the Teensy USB port open** to recover the clean boot banner and confirm the session counter increments from `0` → `sudo sh pi/tools/capture.sh /dev/serial0 30 cap.bin` → `python3 pi/tools/replay.py cap.bin --seconds 30`.
> **Expected: ~1.1 MB, ~25.6 packets/s, zero CRC failures, session established, final state IDLE, return_source TS, and all four jacks UNKNOWN.** Anything else is the finding.
> **Two evidence items still owed that need no new work, only the next power cycle:** the clean boot banner (not captured after the flash — the port can only open once `upload` returns) and proof that the session id changes across a reboot, which is TODO-56's central claim and is currently **unproven on hardware**.
> **⚠️ T1 is no longer a formality.** The link sits at **79 % occupancy**, not the ~41 % the stale documentation implied. That is the number the acceptance matrix exists to interrogate.
> **⬆️ CRITICAL PATH 2026-09-22 (journal (6)) — raised for the 2026-11-01 Maker Portfolio deadline.** The order is now: finish the permanent breadboard → stable firmware/runtime → Teensy↔Pi protocol → Pi production UI → real end-to-end measurement demo → enclosure/presentation, with **Test 4 characterisation as a PARALLEL validation track**. **Nothing here depends on the Test 4 fixture or its capacitor.** **The Pi UI must be driven by REAL Teensy measurements, not canned data.** **A working prototype is not a fully characterised measurement instrument** — keep that distinction in the demo and the portfolio write-up.
**Why:** the Pi 5, Active Cooler, 27 W PSU, Touch Display 2 and Freenove GPIO HAT
were all purchased and the runbook's parts list marks them "used in Phase 9" — but
Phase 9 in that same document is the **validation harness**. The Pi was in the bill
of materials with nowhere to be installed. Retrofitting it into Phase 9 would
repeat exactly the status-collapsing error this file warns against.
**What:** Phase 10 in `docs/breadboard_bringup_v3.md` — Teensy `Serial1`
(pins 0/1) to the Pi UART via the Freenove HAT, level/ground verification before
any data, then link bring-up.
**Proof:** acceptance must test **Teensy UART → Pi → rendered known data
independently of the full analyzer** — a synthetic/known frame rendered correctly
on the Pi panel, plus CRC-rejection behaviour demonstrated by deliberately
corrupting a packet. Do not accept the phase on "the analyzer seems to work".

**🔬 THE T1 ACCEPTANCE MATRIX — this is the decisive test and the harness for it
now ships.** `timing_probe.{h,cpp}`, compiled into **all three** targets (the
control run must use identical code, or the comparison is meaningless). Commands:
`t` starts a measurement window, `T` prints a fixed-format `key=value` report
built to be diffed mechanically.

| Run | Build | Condition | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `_NONE` | no display hardware | **THE CONTROL** |
| 2 | `_PI` | reader consuming normally | normal operation |
| 3 | `_PI` | reader suspended (`kill -STOP`), UART stays full | sustained backpressure |
| 4 | `_PI` | TX pulled mid-RESULT drain | loss mid-transaction |

Identical window length (≥60 s) and identical signal conditions across all four.

**Pass condition — acquisition must not move:**
- `block_ms_mean` / `block_ms_max` / `block_ms_spread` **indistinguishable from run 1**
- `blocks_skipped` / `skip_events` / `largest_block_gap` = **0 in every run**
- `loop_us_max` and `audio_blocks_high_water` comparable to run 1
- Validation suite still runs and passes in run 3

**Transport is ALLOWED to degrade:** drops in runs 3/4 are expected and correct;
`TX room min = 0` in runs 3/4 is backpressure being absorbed as designed;
non-zero abandoned result bytes in run 4 is correct.

**⚠️ DO NOT PASS ON A SMALL CALL TIME.** A microsecond-fast `presentation_*()` can
still disturb the instrument through interrupt load, UART ISR activity, DMA
contention or buffer servicing — none of which appear in a call-duration figure.
The pass condition is the comparison against run 1. The control run *is* the
threshold; there is no absolute number to argue about. This matters more here than
in a typical embedded project because the firmware is built around one capture
owner and one canonical timebase, and everything time-sensitive references it.

Keep all four reports verbatim in journal.md. On pass: T1 closes, protocol v2 goes
from *schema-frozen, transport-unproven* to proven, and the milestone is
**v0.6.0 — Pi presentation architecture validated**.

Closed on SIGTERM and **released the device** — a fresh `capture.sh` immediately afterwards succeeded. **No measurement was initiated**; the 2026-09-27 fixture supplies the RESULT. Record: [`2026-09-30-pi-ui-energised-live-capture.md`](evidence/2026-09-30-pi-ui-energised-live-capture.md).

Source bins 22–25 fall into live bins 5 and 6 at 4:1, giving each `clamped=2, ratio=4`; `data_sender.h` marks saturation only when `clamped == ratio`, so a half-clamped group is **drawn as data, not marked as a rail**. Both figures are correct. **Consequence: runbook step 7's clamp *rendering* contract is still unproven** — it needs a FULLY clamped decimated bin, which this stimulus does not produce.

A renderer willing to fabricate would have drawn something. **Still owed for the portfolio: an energised live TF, which needs the tone restarted.**

> **✅ SLICE 2 2026-09-27 — software only, no firmware change, no bench action. 228 → 252 tests.** Three things, all driven by real hardware bytes rather than by re-reading the header:
> 1. **Discarded bytes are now judged by POSITION, and that was a real hole.** `replay.py` compared the *total* against `max_payload_bytes()` = **10 005 B** and called anything below it "explainable by a mid-stream start" — so up to 10 005 bytes of genuine **interior** loss could pass silently under a figure the report described as a capture artefact, and the bound used a *payload* size where one *framed* packet was the correct quantity. `ScannerStats` now splits leading from interior loss (`bytes_discarded` kept as their sum, so byte accounting and existing readers are unaffected), `replay.py` **fails on any interior discard event**, and `reset()` deliberately charges nothing so a reconnect cannot look like a transport fault. New tool [`pi/tools/byte_audit.py`](../pi/tools/byte_audit.py) closes the accounting and checks inter-frame contiguity, on top of the production scanner rather than a copy of it. **Audit of all five hardware captures: exactly ONE discard event each, at absolute offset 0, zero interior loss, zero inter-frame gaps, zero residual.** Record: [`2026-09-27-discarded-bytes-accounted.md`](evidence/2026-09-27-discarded-bytes-accounted.md).
> 2. **🔴 DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED — the Pi was corrupting Teensy-authored text.** The STATUS packet for `m` carries `"Measuring \xe2\x80\x94 external stimulus"`: a **UTF-8 em dash**, because [guitar_rig_analyzer.ino:560](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L560) has a literal em dash in the source string — as do roughly **20 other `presentation_status()` call sites** and many in `validation.cpp`. The receiver decoded **ASCII**, so every one of them rendered as three U+FFFD replacement characters. Fixed: all Teensy text fields decode UTF-8, `errors="replace"` retained deliberately so a truncated multibyte sequence degrades visibly instead of raising into the frame loop. **The synthesised vectors could never have caught this** — `tests/vectors.py` wrote the same string with an **ASCII hyphen**. Textbook shared-author blind spot, and the second one the hardware stream has broken. Firmware-side contract gap tracked as **TODO-61**.
> 3. **Real-RESULT regression fixtures.** [`tests/test_result_fixture.py`](../pi/tests/test_result_fixture.py) pins the 1 kHz RESULT capture and the timeout capture by SHA-256 and asserts, against bytes a Teensy produced: all four arrays at 512 elements, the per-array NaN conventions (including `coherence` having **zero** NaN — the guard that would catch a receiver turning an unknown bin into 0.0), Teensy-authored metadata, zero interior discard, a closed byte accounting with zero inter-frame gaps, and the UTF-8 status string. Also pins that **no VIEW packet exists** in the RESULT capture, so the absence of link-side `x` recovery stays a recorded fact.
> **🛑 Still not started: the renderer, the Pi-side transport/serial reader, and the UI.** Everything above is parser and evidence hardening. No Phase 10 runbook step is closed by it.
> **✅ SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-26 — `pi/`, software only, no firmware change, no bench action.** Bytes → typed Teensy-authored records: CRC-16/CCITT (`crc.py`), frame scanner with resync and bounded lengths (`framing.py`), decoders for all seven v2 packet types plus `UnknownPacket` (`packets.py`), and a constants/enum mirror (`protocol.py`). **70 tests pass**, Python standard library only — pytest is not installed and no dependency was added. `sh pi/run_tests.sh`.
> **🔺 EVIDENCE BOUND — READ BEFORE CITING THIS.** The test vectors are **synthesised from this repository's reading of `data_sender.h`**. Encoder and decoder share one author and one interpretation, so a misreading of the header would be reproduced identically on both sides and every test would still pass. **What is proven:** the parser is self-consistent with the header, and its CRC rejection, resync, bounded-length and unknown-type behaviour are correct. **What is NOT proven:** that the parser agrees with the **compiled firmware on the wire.** That needs a captured real Teensy byte stream (runbook Phase 10 step 4 onward, hardware). **No Phase 10 step is passed by this work.**
> **Drift guard shipped — the Pi-side half of the audit's acceptance Test C.** `tests/test_firmware_header_sync.py` parses the actual Teensy headers and fails if any mirrored constant, packet id or enum has drifted. The firmware protects its own layout with `static_assert`; nothing protected the Python mirror, and a renumbered enum is the quietest possible protocol break — the packet still parses, the CRC still passes, and the Pi shows the wrong verdict. **Verified by negative control**, not by assumption: mutating `MeasurementState.ABORTED` 6→5, `RESULT_FIXED_BYTES` 156→152, and transposing the coverage nibbles each produced a failing run (journal 2026-09-26 (1)).
> **Two defects found by writing it, neither invented:** the HELLO retransmission gap (**TODO-56**, firmware, gated behind the flash sequence) and the runbook's stale step-12 wording (corrected in this pass — see below).
