This item's own 2026-09-11 status line is the specific rule and resolves it: **parser now, renderer after T1.** Step 12's wording was the stale half and is corrected to say renderer.
> **Still 🔴 and untouched:** receiver state model (measurement state machine, sticky `ABORTED`, HELLO lifecycle); serial transport and capture/replay tooling; **the renderer, which stays blocked on T1**; and the Pi→Teensy command path, which does **not** come through the presentation boundary ([presentation.h](../presentation.h)) — it is CONTROL and needs its own validated input surface.
> **UI framework: still undecided, deliberately.** No framework appears anywhere in the tree and none was introduced here. Per the standing rule it gets an explicit ADR when the renderer starts, not a choice buried in an unrelated change.
**Status 2026-09-11:** protocol v2 is **schema-frozen** — field positions, packet
type ids, enum meanings, clamp/coverage semantics and the RESULT layout will not
change without a defect and a version bump. The parser can be written now.

**Read `live_bins` and `decim_ratio` from the HELLO packet rather than hardcoding
them.** Cadence, bin count, TX buffering, chunking and baud are explicitly NOT
frozen — Phase 10's T1 measurement may change any of them — and a receiver that
takes its geometry from HELLO is immune to all of it. One that hardcodes 128 bins
will break the first time the cadence lever is pulled.

**Do not start the renderer/UI until T1 passes.** Building views on a transport not
yet shown to be harmless to measurement timing is the one dependency worth
respecting here; the parser is safe to build because the schema is frozen, but the
renderer embeds assumptions about frame rate and latency that T1 could invalidate.
**Why:** ADR-001 accepts a second codebase as a real cost. None of it exists yet.
**What:** receiver + framing scanner; packet parser; CRC rejection and resync;
reconnect/stall behaviour (the Teensy may reboot mid-stream); measurement state
model mirroring the firmware's `IDLE → ARMED → CAPTURE → PROCESSING → RESULT`
plus sticky `ABORTED`; live spectrum/TF view; completed-measurement view showing
quality **and** provenance; touch navigation; then Compare/Recall and the radial
tone fingerprint.
**Constraint — non-negotiable (ADR-001):** the Pi must not compute anything that
determines whether a measurement is valid. Confidence, coherence, clamp state and
quality grade arrive as verdicts and are displayed, never recomputed. Interpolating
pixels, animating, formatting and navigating are all fine.
**Proof:** a held-out recorded packet stream replays to identical rendered output;
a deliberately corrupted packet is rejected, not rendered; a mid-stream Teensy
reboot recovers without a restart.

### TODO-56 — HELLO is sent once, best-effort, and never retransmitted — a Pi that starts late can never decode a RESULT 🟡 **FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-09-26 — compile-verified `_PI` and `_NONE`, NOT FLASHED, NOT BENCH-VERIFIED**
> **✅ Implemented: Option A — periodic best-effort re-announcement, 1 Hz, gated on `s_result_len == 0` only.** Decision approved 2026-09-26 after the inbound-command alternative was evidence-checked and rejected for this slice. **Deliberately NOT gated on `MeasurementState::IDLE`:** HELLO describes the instrument and protocol session, not the measurement. A Pi reconnecting during ARMED, CAPTURE, PROCESSING, RESULT, EXPORTING — or during a **sticky ABORTED**, which is latched until `measurementEngine_acknowledgeAbort()` — must be able to obtain geometry and reconstruct that state rather than sit geometry-blind waiting for IDLE.
> **🛑 Option B (Pi→Teensy request) was REJECTED FOR THIS SLICE, not dismissed.** It requires the firmware's **first externally-controlled input surface**, which [presentation.h](../presentation.h) explicitly refuses to host (*"IF Pi → TEENSY INPUT IS EVER ADDED, IT DOES NOT COME THROUGH HERE"*) and which needs its own validated boundary and its own ADR. Its real value is the eventual touch command channel, which Option A neither blocks nor complicates. **Two hazards recorded against it:** `Serial1.begin()` enables RX and nothing drains it today, and Raspberry Pi OS ships a serial console on that UART by default — a request parser would be reading console garbage as input. **The console hazard is now a [STOP] in runbook Phase 10 step 2**, configured while the consequence is still zero.
> **🔑 `session_id` — HELLO bytes `[18-19]`, previously "reserved (0)".** Periodic announcement alone leaves an ambiguity it does not solve: repeated HELLOs are indistinguishable from a **new Teensy session**. `output_block_number` cannot substitute — it is 16 bits and wraps on its own roughly every 50 minutes, so "it went backwards" is not sound evidence of a reboot. Contract: **constant for one boot, changed after a restart; a CHANGED value is authoritative evidence that prior session state is stale.** Not security, not measurement identity, not device identity.
> **Generation mechanism — chosen on evidence, with the rejects recorded so nobody "simplifies" it back.** Four platform facilities were checked in the Teensy 1.62.0 core before anything was written:
>
> | Facility | Verdict |
> |---|---|
> | `SNVS_LPGPR0..3` | Survives a **warm** reset, but Teensy 4.1 has no coin cell on this domain — a **power cycle clears it**, so two consecutive cold boots report the same value. Rejected |
> | `rtc_get()` / `Teensy3Clock` | Same power domain, same weakness without a VBAT cell. Rejected |
> | `ARM_DWT_CYCCNT` / `micros()` at init | Read at a fixed point in a fixed boot sequence, so **very nearly deterministic**. This is the "weak random mechanism" the decision forbade — and worse, it would *look* random while colliding often. Rejected |
> | `HW_OCOTP_MAC0` | The chip serial. Constant by definition — identifies the **board**, not the session, so it cannot discriminate a reboot at all. Rejected (it is the right field for device identity, which HELLO does not carry) |
>
> **Selected: a monotonic `uint16_t` boot counter in the Teensy 4.1's flash-emulated EEPROM** — the only candidate satisfying the contract in *every* reboot mode including a cold power cycle, and deterministic, which is what makes runbook step 10 a **test** rather than an observation. Address reserved in `config.h` as `EEPROM_ADDR_LINK_SESSION`, **globally** — `data_sender.cpp` compiles only for `DISPLAY_TARGET_PI`, so without a global reservation a future EEPROM user under another target would collide and the symptom would be a Pi that believes the Teensy rebooted whenever some unrelated subsystem wrote its state.
> **Documented limitations (also in `data_sender.cpp`):** 16-bit, wraps after 65535 boots (harmless — the contract is "changed ⇒ stale", not "ordered"); **not unique across devices**, two Teensys can both be at boot 42; predictable by design, never a token; counts `_PI` boots only, so it is not an instrument-wide boot count; a blank EEPROM's first boot reads `0xFFFF` and becomes `0`, and **0 is a legitimate value a receiver must not treat as "absent"**.
> **Cost and placement:** one 2-byte EEPROM write per boot — the firmware's only flash write. `dataSender_init()` runs at [.ino:311](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L311), **before** `audioCap_init()` at :323, so no audio is running and there is no acquisition timing to perturb. HELLO is 47 B framed ≈ **1.02 ms** of wire time at 460800 baud; at 1 Hz that is ~0.1 % of the link against the ~40 % live frames already occupy.
> **Separate HELLO statistics — `hello_sent` / `hello_dropped`, NOT folded into `events_dropped`.** Re-announcement is *expected* to drop under sustained backpressure; that is the policy working. In T1 run 3 a suspended reader keeps the UART full by design, so every second would add a HELLO drop and the existing event figure would stop meaning anything. **This changes the T1 report's content — the four runs must be captured against this build, not compared to any earlier report.**
> **🔺 EVIDENCE — compile only.** `_PI` and `_NONE` both build clean with **zero warnings**; `_RA8875` still stops on its **pre-existing** `AUDIO_SHIELD_STACKED` `#error` (untouched, not a regression). 79 offline Pi tests pass, including six new `session_id` tests and two new drift-guard checks, each verified by negative control. **None of this is wire evidence.** The parser and the firmware still share one author's reading of the header; agreement is established by a captured real Teensy stream. **NOT FLASHED. T1 remains required.**
> **⚠️ Binary equivalence of the `_NONE` control cannot be proven by hash.** Building HEAD twice produces two different hex md5s — [.ino:215](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L215) stamps `__DATE__`/`__TIME__`, by design (TODO-52/53). What *was* verified: `_NONE` section sizes are **byte-identical** before and after this change (`code:152448 data:72200 headers:8820`), and `data_sender.cpp` is entirely `#if`'d out for `_NONE` while the `config.h` addition is a `#define` emitting no code. **Corollary worth carrying forward: "rebuild the commit and check the md5 matches" is NOT a valid identity procedure.** The recorded md5 identifies a flashed *artifact*, not a source revision.
**Remaining to close:** flash as part of the TODO-9 intermediate build, positively identify it, then demonstrate **both** directions of runbook step 10 — Teensy reset (`session_id` changes) and **Pi restart with the Teensy untouched** (`session_id` unchanged, geometry re-obtained within ~1 s, next RESULT decodes). The second direction is the one that was previously impossible and that the runbook never tested.
**Proof:** with the Teensy running and undisturbed, a Pi receiver started fresh obtains protocol geometry and decodes a RESULT without the Teensy being reset; and a Teensy reset is distinguished from a re-announcement by a changed `session_id`.

<details><summary><b>Original finding, retained for the reasoning trail (2026-09-26)</b></summary>

**Why it matters:** HELLO (`0x06`) is the **only** carrier of `fft_bins`, and a RESULT packet's four `float[fft_bins]` arrays **cannot be located without it** — the `insight_count` that would let a receiver solve for the length sits *after* the arrays, so the geometry is genuinely not recoverable from the packet. HELLO is therefore not an optional greeting; it is the decode key.
**What was found (`OBS`, source):** `dataSender_init()` builds and sends HELLO exactly once ([data_sender.cpp:263](../data_sender.cpp#L263)) via `timedBestEffort()`, which **drops the packet and increments `events_dropped` if the TX buffer is full**. There is no retransmission, no periodic re-announce, and no request path — `Serial1` RX is reserved but unused, so the Pi cannot ask for one.
**Two failure modes, both `INFER` from that code — neither has been observed on hardware:**
1. **Pi starts after the Teensy.** The Teensy announced itself to nobody. The Pi sees live frames it can decode (LIVE_FRAME is self-describing from its payload length) but **every RESULT is undecodable, indefinitely.**
2. **That single HELLO is dropped.** Same outcome, from one full buffer at boot — and the only trace is a counter.
**Why runbook step 10 does not catch it:** step 10 resets the **Teensy** while the Pi receives, which re-runs `init()` and resends HELLO. That direction works. **Pi-restart recovery is not tested by it and is not provable against the current firmware.**
**What (proposed, NOT implemented — firmware-visible behaviour, gated behind the TODO-9 flash/identify sequence):** re-announce HELLO periodically while no measurement is running, or on a Pi→Teensy request once a control channel exists. A periodic re-announce is cheap (40 B at ~1 Hz) and must stay on the same non-blocking best-effort path — **this fix must not become the first blocking write in the super-loop.** Decide the mechanism before Phase 10 step 10 is attempted.
**Pi-side handling already in place:** `packets.decode_result()` raises `GeometryUnknown` rather than guessing a length, so the receiver fails loudly instead of mis-slicing an array. That is containment, not a fix — the data is still undecodable.
**Proof:** with the Teensy running and undisturbed, a Pi receiver started fresh obtains protocol geometry and decodes a RESULT without the Teensy being reset.

</details>

### TODO-59 — A best-effort packet written during a RESULT drain lands INSIDE the result and destroys it 🟡 **FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-09-26 — transport atomicity invariant enforced centrally. FLASHED AND RUN 2026-09-27; the POSITIVE CONTENTION CASE IS STILL UNEXERCISED**
> **🛑 2026-09-27 — a real RESULT drained on hardware and the guard was never asked to refuse anything.** `26f2bee` staged and drained one RESULT over **102 ms** and reported `Atomicity: 0 best-effort packets refused while a RESULT was draining`. The RESULT arrived complete and CRC-valid at the Pi, so **no contention occurred** — not *was handled*. **`blocked_by_result == 0` proves the counter is wired, not that the invariant works.** What would prove it: a best-effort producer firing inside a known drain window, with `blocked_by_result` rising and the RESULT still arriving intact. **Do not manufacture that collision without a written test plan** — the useful version of this test also has to show the refused packet's own loss being accounted for, which is why it is a design question and not a one-liner.
> **⚠️ `TX room min: 0 B of 4096 B` observed in the same window, and preserved rather than dismissed.** Simultaneously true: RESULT complete, CRC valid, **zero** packets of any class dropped, block period 46.7 ms against 46.4 expected. The T1 matrix expects `TX room min = 0` in runs 3/4 as backpressure absorbed by design, but this was run-2-like conditions where it was not predicted. The staged drain filling the UART as fast as it will take bytes is the obvious candidate — **candidate, not conclusion**: the figure is a min-since-boot high-water mark and nothing timestamps it against the drain.
> **⬆️ Found during the TODO-57 design pass and fixed FIRST, as an isolated change.** It is independently discovered, independently testable, and it protects every future packet producer — including TODO-57's STATE packet and TODO-58's ROUTER_STATUS, neither of which could safely ship without it.

**The defect (`OBS` for mechanism, `INFER` for reachability — not observed on hardware).**
`dataSender_service()` drains a staged RESULT in chunks across loop iterations, writing straight into the UART ring. `trySendBestEffort()` wrote to the same ring with **no result-pending interlock** — only a `txRoom()` check. Anything emitted between two drain passes therefore landed **inside** the RESULT's byte stream. The receiver reads the interleaved packet as RESULT payload, fails the CRC, and **the whole result is lost — while `results_sent` still counts it as sent.** Silent data loss on the path that exists precisely to be reliable, presenting as a transport fault.

`dataSender_sendResult()` already knew the hazard and guarded only RESULT-vs-RESULT ([data_sender.cpp:539-545](../data_sender.cpp#L539)): *"interleaving two packets would corrupt both."* The same reasoning was never applied to event packets.

**Every RESULT is exposed.** Minimum framed RESULT is 8356 B against a 4096 B TX buffer, so every result needs ≥3 drain passes; worst case 10012 B ≈ 217 ms.

**🔺 THE SMALL PACKET IS THE DANGEROUS ONE — this is counter-intuitive and is why it survived review.** After `dataSender_service()` returns, the loop has exited with *either* the result complete *or* `txRoom() == 0`, so a large packet is refused by the room check. But the UART keeps draining at ~46 B/ms, so a few dozen bytes are free by the time another producer runs. A **12-byte STATUS (`"Ready"`) fits; a 1689-byte live frame does not.** Size is not protection.
**Most reachable path:** the clip-status messages at [guitar_rig_analyzer.ino:759-771](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L759) sit **outside** the live-display gate, so `presentation_status()` fires during a RESULT drain whenever the player is still making noise — which is exactly when a measurement has just completed.

**✅ Fix — enforced CENTRALLY, as a transport invariant, not per producer:**
> **While a staged RESULT is draining, no independently framed best-effort packet may write bytes to the stream.**

Implemented in `trySendBestEffort()`, which **all six** best-effort packet types pass through — LIVE_FRAME included, via its own call at [data_sender.cpp:510](../data_sender.cpp#L510). Gating individual producers would have worked until the next packet type was added by someone who did not know to do it; **an invariant that can be forgotten is not an invariant.**

**Caller audit performed before the central guard was written — no exceptions found.** All six are best-effort by documented contract: HELLO (self-heals in ≤1 s since TODO-56), LIVE_FRAME (droppable/latest-wins, and blocked from RESULT state by the display gate anyway), STATUS (display text), VIEW (*"a dropped view packet is recoverable"*), ABORTED, ROUTER_STATUS (no callers, TODO-58). **Nuance recorded:** ABORTED and VIEW justify their droppability with the claim TODO-57 proved false, so their *policy* is best-effort while their *safety net* does not exist yet. Dropping them still strictly beats corrupting the RESULT, which loses both the result and the event.

**The drain cannot stall**, so the refusal window is bounded: the UART transmits whether or not a Pi is listening, so a staged result always drains in ≤~217 ms.

**The HELLO gate in `dataSender_service()` is RETAINED, and is no longer a duplicate safety guard.** Its documented purpose changed: it avoids building a 40-byte payload and its CRC only to have them refused, and it keeps `hello_dropped` meaning *backpressure* rather than accumulating a drop every second of every RESULT drain — a structural condition, not a link problem. The safety now comes from the central invariant. Comment updated to say so.

**New counter `blocked_by_result`, reported by `u`.** A non-zero value **is the invariant working, not a fault**; expect it to rise whenever a result is delivered while the signal is live.

**📌 `results_sent` semantics made explicit — it was quietly implying something it cannot know.** It means *"every byte of the framed RESULT was accepted into the UART over time."* There is no return path and no acknowledgement in this protocol, so the Teensy cannot know whether a receiver got it or whether it passed CRC. **It is a transmitter statistic and must never be cited as end-to-end delivery evidence** — before this fix, a RESULT could be corrupted on the wire and still counted here as sent. Documented at the field.

**🔺 EVIDENCE — compile and offline only.** `_PI` and `_NONE` compile with **zero warnings**. 123 Pi tests pass (113 → 123). Ten new tests in two tiers, and the tiers are not interchangeable:
- **Consumer regression tests (`OBS`)** — the discovered stream shape is constructed and replayed: the corrupted RESULT is rejected rather than rendered, the spliced packet itself still arrives (which is how the defect would present on a bench — status text updating normally while measurements silently fail to appear), and the stream recovers. A clean-result control prevents these passing for the wrong reason.
- **Source structural guards (`OBS` of source, NOT of execution)** — the invariant is present in `trySendBestEffort()`, precedes any UART write, is counted, and **exactly two functions in the file write to the UART**. That last one is the coverage check: a future direct writer would bypass the choke point entirely.
**NOT proven: that the firmware guard executes correctly.** That needs a captured Teensy stream with a RESULT delivered while the signal is live — bench work. **NOT FLASHED.**
**Proof:** a RESULT delivered while clip-status transitions are firing arrives intact at the Pi and passes CRC; `blocked_by_result` is non-zero for that run.

### TODO-57 — Measurement state is not observable to a reconnecting Pi, and the documented safety net for a dropped ABORTED is FALSE 🟡 **FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-09-26 — `DS_PACKET_STATE` 0x08, periodic at 500 ms. Compile-verified `_PI` and `_NONE`, NOT FLASHED, NOT BENCH-VERIFIED**
> **✅ Implemented: periodic-only authoritative STATE snapshot, 500 ms, best-effort, through the existing choke point.** Payload: `measurement_state`, `abort_reason`, 2 reserved, `audio_block_counter`. 8 bytes, 15 framed, ~0.33 ms of wire time — ~0.07 % duty.
> **Periodic-only, NOT transition-triggered — decided on call-site evidence, and the evidence inverts the intuition.** `measurement_engine.cpp` has **16** `transitionTo()` call sites but `update()` ever returns only **5** non-`NONE` events, and several transitions (`arm`, `returnToLive`, `acknowledgeAbort`, `exportToSD`) happen inside command functions that never pass through `update()` at all. `MeasurementEvent::STATE_CHANGED` exists and the `.ino` **never handles it**. Transition-triggered announcement would mean wiring ~11 currently-silent transitions across two call paths, and **its failure mode is a missing announcement — precisely the defect being fixed.** A periodic snapshot reads the authoritative getter and needs none of that plumbing.
> **✅ Coherent pair — verified before implementing, not assumed.** There is no combined snapshot API; `measurementEngine_getState()` and `getAbortReason()` are separate. They are nonetheless coherent for **two independent reasons**: (a) the engine maintains the pairing itself — `abortTo()` sets the reason **before** `transitionTo(ABORTED)`, `acknowledgeAbort()` clears it **before** `transitionTo(IDLE)`, and `arm()` clears it — so `IDLE + CLIP_SOURCE` is unconstructible; and (b) `measurement_engine.cpp` has **no interrupt path at all** — no ISR, no `volatile`, no `attachInterrupt` — so nothing can transition between two adjacent reads in loop context. **No API change was needed. If the engine is ever driven from a timer or ISR, re-check (b);** the comment in `sendState()` says so.
> **🔺 `audio_block_counter` IS A TIMESTAMP, NOT A SEQUENCE NUMBER — and this is not merely conservative.** `audioCap_getBlockCounter()` returns `(uint32_t)(micros() * SAMPLE_RATE / (AUDIO_BLOCK_SAMPLES * 1e6))`, so it **wraps to zero roughly every 71.6 minutes** and never exceeds ~1.48e6. It is **not** a monotonic 32-bit counter, and a newer-than comparison against it would be **wrong**, not just unproven. The Pi records it and implements no comparison logic. Ordering is already guaranteed by the transport: `trySendBestEffort()` writes whole packets in FIFO order and nothing can reorder them.
> **No RESULT-pending guard was added to STATE, deliberately.** TODO-59 owns transport atomicity centrally; duplicating the check per producer is what made TODO-59 possible. The consequence is recorded rather than hidden: **`state_dropped` counts both causes of refusal** — no UART room, and the atomicity invariant. `blocked_by_result` disambiguates at the aggregate level, and the diagnostics line says so.
> **✅ The false comments are corrected, both of them.** [data_sender.cpp](../data_sender.cpp)'s ABORTED **and** VIEW notes both claimed *"measurement_state rides in every live frame"*; both now name the STATE packet as the safety net and state why the live frame never could be. TODO-34's mitigation clause is corrected in the same pass.
> **🔺 EVIDENCE — compile and offline only.** `_PI` and `_NONE` compile with **zero warnings**. 138 Pi tests pass (123 → 138), including: every previously-unobservable state (CAPTURE/PROCESSING/RESULT/EXPORTING) now reaching the model; a **late attach learning a sticky ABORTED with no ABORTED event ever sent**; a dropped ABORTED self-healing; `abort_reason` legitimately `None` while `abort_reason_code` is known; wire-position pinning for all four fields. Negative controls: drifting the firmware cadence fails the sync test; making STATE stop latching on ABORTED fails two tests. **NOT FLASHED. Wire agreement unproven.**
**✅ PARTIALLY BENCH-VERIFIED 2026-09-26 (Step A).** Flashed as `26f2bee` and exercised over the real Teensy→Pi link. **The primary result is a byte-offset packet census, not a counter reading:** following the ABORTED packet at offset **343 845** of [`phase10-stepA-abort.bin`](evidence/phase10-stepA-abort.bin), the remaining **4 025 bytes contain 71 STATE, 35 HELLO, 35 ROUTER_STATUS and ZERO LIVE_FRAME packets.** All 71 **subsequent** STATE snapshots carry `ABORTED` with `abort_reason TIMEOUT`; **none of those 71 carries `NONE`** — the single `NONE` interval belongs to the ABORTED packet itself, before the next snapshot (see the 502 ms lag below). Sustained ~35 s. Record: [`2026-09-26-phase10-stepA-abort.md`](evidence/2026-09-26-phase10-stepA-abort.md), journal 2026-09-26 (12).
> **The claim this supports, precisely worded:** *without STATE, the Pi would have continued receiving link traffic but lacked authoritative measurement-state telemetry telling it the Teensy was latched in ABORTED(TIMEOUT).* The 70 continuing HELLO/ROUTER packets are why the stronger phrasing "silence indistinguishable from a dead link" is **wrong and withdrawn** — the link was demonstrably healthy. **Link health and measurement state are separable; only STATE carries the latter.**
> **Two-stage abort information confirmed on hardware:** the ABORTED packet carries the prose (`"armed timeout"`) and sets the latch with `reason_code NONE`; the machine-readable `TIMEOUT` arrives **502 ms later — exactly one STATE announce period** — with the next snapshot.
> **Also confirmed:** LIVE_FRAME flows throughout ARMED (202 packets ≈22/s), matching the firmware's `IDLE | ARMED` display gate, observed from the receiving end.
**Remaining to close:** a Pi **attaching during** a sticky ABORTED (it was attached throughout this run); a **deliberately suppressed or lost** ABORTED event still leaving the Pi able to establish ABORTED with the right reason code; and a Pi attached during **CAPTURE**, which this run never entered (no stimulus, so ARMED timed out instead).

<details><summary><b>Original finding, retained for the reasoning trail (2026-09-26)</b></summary>

> **⬆️ This should be the next producer-side slice, ahead of the renderer.** Building UI on a state channel that cannot represent a sticky ABORTED after reconnect means the defect becomes invisible at exactly the moment it matters.

**Two findings, one root cause — the only carrier of measurement state is gated off in most states.**

**F1 — `LIVE_FRAME` is only sent in `IDLE` or `ARMED`** (`OBS`, [guitar_rig_analyzer.ino:748-756](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L748)). The `live_display_active` gate suppresses live updates in CAPTURE, PROCESSING, RESULT, EXPORTING and **ABORTED**, so the `measurement_state` byte at LIVE_FRAME offset [0] is effectively a **two-value field**.
**➡️ Therefore the safety net documented at [data_sender.cpp:681](../data_sender.cpp#L681) does not hold.** It claims *"measurement_state rides in every live frame, so the Pi still learns the fact"*, and **TODO-34 cites exactly that as the mitigation for a dropped ABORTED**. During ABORTED there are no live frames at all. If the best-effort ABORTED packet is dropped, **the Pi never learns the abort happened** — it sees live frames stop and later resume showing IDLE. The claim must be corrected wherever it appears, not just the code fixed.

**F2 — a reconnecting Pi cannot learn the current measurement state at all.** HELLO carries geometry and capabilities, not measurement state. Combined with F1, a Pi attaching during a sticky ABORTED receives HELLO and then **silence**. Absence of live frames is ambiguous between *"not IDLE/ARMED"*, *"link dead"* and *"Teensy wedged"*. **This is TODO-56's exact shape one layer up:** state announced once as an event, with no way to re-obtain it. TODO-56 fixed it for geometry; measurement state still has it.

**🛑 DO NOT FIX F1 BY WIDENING THE LIVE_FRAME GATE.** Sending live frames through CAPTURE/PROCESSING/RESULT/ABORTED merely to transport one state byte couples **state observability to DSP/live-stream availability** — two things that must be able to fail independently. LIVE_FRAME is a live-data product; it is not a state channel. The likely correct shape is a small authoritative state/status packet, or a periodic state announcement on the TODO-56 pattern. **That deserves its own design pass and its own decision gate.**

**Scope this must cover when designed:**
- late attachment and reconnect during **every** measurement state, not just IDLE;
- a dropped ABORTED event;
- **sticky ABORTED**, which is latched until `measurementEngine_acknowledgeAbort()` ([measurement_engine.h:167](../measurement_engine.h#L167)) and must survive a reconnect as a fact about the instrument;
- an unambiguous distinction between **link silence** and **measurement state**.

**✅ A transport precondition for this item is now met (TODO-59).** The design gate identified that any new state packet must not interleave with a draining RESULT. That is no longer TODO-57's problem to remember: the invariant is enforced centrally in `trySendBestEffort()`, so the STATE packet inherits it by construction.

**Consumer-side containment already in place (slice 3, not a fix):** the Pi receiver models `UNKNOWN` as a first-class state and never maps silence, HELLO, connection or session establishment to IDLE. That represents the gap honestly; it does not close it.
**Proof:** a Pi receiver attached at an arbitrary moment — including mid-CAPTURE and during a sticky ABORTED — obtains the current measurement state without the Teensy being reset and without inferring it from the absence of packets; and a deliberately dropped ABORTED still leaves the Pi able to establish that the instrument is in ABORTED.

</details>

</details>

### TODO-60 — `ENABLE_MONITOR_MODE 1` does not compile: `RouterStatus` has no `monitor_mode` member 🔴 **latent build break found 2026-09-26 during the TODO-58 design pass. Separate from TODO-58 by decision — this is feature repair, not protocol truthfulness**
**What (`OBS`):** [data_sender.cpp:747](../data_sender.cpp#L747) reads `rs.monitor_mode` inside `#if ENABLE_MONITOR_MODE`. **`RouterStatus` has no such member** ([signal_router.h:118-140](../signal_router.h#L118)) — the field list is `jacks`, `return_source`, `injection_active`, `route_valid`, `can_inject`, `description`. Monitor mode exists only as `static MonitorMode s_monitor_mode` in [signal_router.cpp:65](../signal_router.cpp#L65), with a setter at :424 and **no accessor on the public status struct.**
**Why it is invisible:** `ENABLE_MONITOR_MODE` is **0** ([config.h:327](../config.h#L327)), so the reference is preprocessed away and the tree compiles clean. Setting the flag to `1` fails the build.
**🔁 This is the SAME TRAP TODO-9 already documents for injection:** *"flipping the flag to 1 fails to LINK rather than enabling injection"* — `audioCap_setInjectionMode()` is declared only in the staging header `audiocap_injection_api.h`. **Two feature flags in this firmware now break the build rather than enabling their feature.** Worth treating as a pattern rather than two coincidences: a compile-time flag whose disabled path is the only one ever built is an untested code path by construction.
**What:** add a `MonitorMode monitor_mode` member to `RouterStatus` populated by `updateStatus()` from `s_monitor_mode`, or a `signalRouter_getMonitorMode()` accessor for `data_sender` to call. **Do not redesign monitor mode here** — Phase B is not implemented in v0.6.0 and this item is only about the flag being honest.
**Not a blocker for TODO-58:** with the feature disabled, `s_monitor_mode` is `SOURCE_RETURN` and the corrected ROUTER_STATUS transmitting `monitor_mode = 0` is **truthful**, not a placeholder. HELLO's capability bit already tells the Pi the feature is not compiled in, so it will not render a control that cannot respond (audit T4).
**Consider while fixing:** a compile check for the enabled path. Both this and TODO-9's injection flag would have been caught by building each feature flag's enabled arm once.
**Proof:** `ENABLE_MONITOR_MODE 1` compiles clean for `DISPLAY_TARGET_PI`, and ROUTER_STATUS carries the router's actual monitor mode.

### TODO-63 — The T1 window resets acquisition stats but NOT transport stats, so the four runs' `[Link]` columns overlap 🔴 **found 2026-09-27 while preparing the T1 run sheet. Acceptance-integrity defect, no runtime fault**
**What (`OBS`, from source):** `timingProbe_reset()` ([timing_probe.cpp:45](../timing_probe.cpp#L45)) clears only the `[T1]` accumulators. **`dataSender_resetStats()` is called exactly once, from `dataSender_init()`** ([data_sender.cpp:455](../data_sender.cpp#L455)) — nowhere else, and not from the `t` command. So the `[T1]` block is **window-scoped** while the `[Link]` block is **boot-scoped and cumulative since power-on**, and `tx_room_min` is a min-since-boot high-water mark.
**Why it matters for T1 specifically:** runs 2, 3 and 4 all use `_PI`. Executed in one boot, run 3's drop counts would silently include run 2's, and once `tx_room_min` reaches 0 in run 3 it reads 0 in run 4 permanently. **The transport columns of the acceptance matrix would not mean what the matrix says they mean** — and nothing in the report announces the different scopes, so the figures look directly comparable.
**🔑 This also explains the 2026-09-27 `TX room min: 0 B of 4096 B` observation.** It was min-since-boot from a session that had already drained a full RESULT and sent 4 846 live frames. **Nothing timestamped it against the drain**, which is exactly why that observation was recorded as a candidate rather than a conclusion. The figure is not wrong; it answers a different question than it appears to.
**Workaround adopted for T1, no firmware change:** **reboot between every run.** Free, needs no reflash, resets the `[Link]` block, and increments the session id — so `pi/tools/t1_diff.py` can *detect* two runs sharing a boot by a repeated session id and flag it. Recorded in [`docs/T1_run_sheet.md`](../docs/T1_run_sheet.md).
**What (the real fix):** either have `timingProbe_reset()` also reset the transport stats, or give the report a second window-scoped transport snapshot alongside the cumulative one. **Prefer the second** — the cumulative figures are genuinely useful for "has this boot ever dropped anything", and destroying them to get a window view trades one blind spot for another. Whichever is chosen, **the report must label the scope of every figure it prints**, because an unlabelled scope is what made this invisible.
**Do not fix by reflashing before T1.** The board runs `26f2bee` and the reboot workaround is sufficient; changing the image to improve its own instrumentation mid-acceptance would invalidate the artifact the matrix is being run against.
**Proof:** two consecutive windows in one boot produce transport figures that are each window-scoped, or are labelled cumulative; and the T1 reports carry an unambiguous scope for every line.

### TODO-64 — `TX room min` prints the never-sampled sentinel as `0`, collapsing "unknown" into a meaningful value 🔴 **found 2026-09-27, same pass as TODO-63. Low severity, direct violation of the project's own representation rule**
**What (`OBS`):** [data_sender.cpp:1002](../data_sender.cpp#L1002) prints `(s.tx_room_min == 0xFFFFFFFFu) ? 0UL : (unsigned long)s.tx_room_min`. `dataSender_resetStats()` initialises `tx_room_min` to `0xFFFFFFFFu` as a "never sampled" sentinel ([data_sender.cpp:946](../data_sender.cpp#L946)). **So a never-sampled counter and a genuinely observed zero print identically** — and `0` is the single most alarming value this field can show, since the line itself annotates it *"(0 = the Pi is not draining)"*.
**Why it matters even though it is currently unreachable in practice:** `txRoom()` is called on every send decision, so in any `_PI` build with traffic the sentinel is always overwritten and today's reading was real. But **the whole architecture rests on "unknown/invalid/clamped values must not silently become zero"** — the rule that produced ROUTER_STATUS's `jack_detect_available` mask (TODO-58) and the RESULT NaN conventions. This is the same mistake in a diagnostic, and diagnostics are what bench evidence is written from. It becomes reachable the moment anything prints diagnostics before the first send — a future early-boot diagnostic, or a build where the UART is configured but idle.
**What:** print a distinguishable token for the sentinel — `TX room min:  not sampled` — rather than a number. One line, no behaviour change, no new field.
**Not a T1 blocker.** Recorded in the run sheet as a reading caveat: do not treat `TX room min: 0` as evidence in a run with zero `send_calls`.
**Proof:** a report emitted before any send prints `not sampled`; a report after a send prints the observed minimum.

### TODO-65 — RESULT carries no per-bin clamp mask, so a railed bin is indistinguishable from a measured one 🔴 **found 2026-09-29 while building the Pi UI. TODO-29's defect class, on the RESULT surface**
**What (`OBS`, from the real 2026-09-27 RESULT):** `tf_cal_mag_db` has exactly **four** finite bins of 512 (indices 22–25, 947.5–1076.7 Hz) and **all four read exactly −24.0 dB — the firmware's clamp rail.** Nothing in the RESULT packet says so.
**The asymmetry, stated precisely:** LIVE_FRAME carries **per-bin coverage nibbles** (clamped/valid) *and* a frame-level `tf_clamped_count` — that was audit finding **L1**, closed in protocol v2 precisely so a consumer could not draw a rail as a measurement. **RESULT carries neither.** `Result` has `clip_source_count` / `clip_return_count` (input clipping) but no TF clamp representation at all.
**Why it matters:** this is the exact defect protocol v2 exists to prevent, one packet type over. On 2026-09-07 a consumer printed a flat, confident **−24.0 dB** curve in 33 of 33 windows that was entirely a clamp rail (TODO-29). A Pi RESULT view has no way to avoid repeating it — the numbers are real, finite, and wrong to believe.
**What the UI does in the meantime (not a fix):** [`analyzer_ui/app.py`](../pi/analyzer_ui/app.py) detects finite bins sitting on the −24.0 dB rail and prints, on the plot: *"all N finite bin(s) sit on the −24.0 dB clamp rail · RESULT carries no per-bin clamp mask, so measured and railed are indistinguishable here"*, cross-referencing the live frame's `tf_clamped_count` when one is available. **That is an honest admission, not a substitute for the data.** Detecting the rail by comparing against −24.0 is a heuristic that would break the moment the clamp constant changed, and it cannot distinguish a genuine −24.0 dB measurement from a rail — which is the whole problem.
**What:** carry the clamp information in RESULT. Options, to be decided rather than assumed: a per-bin coverage array mirroring LIVE_FRAME's nibbles (+512 B); a frame-level `tf_clamped_count` plus a bitmap (+64 B); or the count alone (cheapest, weakest — it says *how many* but not *which*). **The bitmap is probably the right trade**, but the RESULT is already 8 901 B and T1 has not been adjudicated, so packet growth is not a free decision.
**⚠️ Protocol-version consequence:** any of these changes the RESULT payload shape and therefore requires a `PI_PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump and a coordinated Pi-side change. Do not slip it in under v2.
**Pinned by a test:** `pi/tests/test_ui.py::test_result_carries_no_per_bin_clamp_mask` asserts the gap exists, so it fails loudly — in the right place — when the protocol closes it.
**Proof:** a RESULT whose railed bins are marked as saturation rather than plotted as measurements, with the Pi rendering them distinctly; and the heuristic in `app.py` deleted in the same pass.

### TODO-61 — The wire contract never states a text encoding, and the firmware's own strings are UTF-8 🟡 **Pi side fixed 2026-09-27; the contract and the RA8875 path are not**
**What (`OBS`, from real hardware bytes):** `data_sender.h` documents STATUS (0x03), ABORTED (0x04) and ROUTER_STATUS's description as strings with a length cap (`DS_MAX_STRING_PAYLOAD 200`) and **says nothing about their encoding**. The firmware emits **UTF-8**: the STATUS message for `m` is `"Measuring \xe2\x80\x94 external stimulus"`, and an em dash appears in ~20 `presentation_status()` calls in [guitar_rig_analyzer.ino](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino) plus several in `validation.cpp`. The Pi decoded ASCII and produced replacement characters (fixed 2026-09-27, TODO-36 slice 2).
**Why it matters beyond the one fix:** an unstated encoding is a contract gap, not a receiver bug — the next consumer repeats the mistake, and a **fixed-width or capped field can truncate a multibyte sequence**, so "valid UTF-8" is not guaranteed even when the author's string was. The Pi now degrades such a byte to a visible replacement character; nothing forces a future consumer to.
**Also unverified: the RA8875 debug path.** `presentation_status()` feeds the same literals to the Adafruit GFX text renderer, whose built-in font is 7-bit. **Whether an em dash renders as junk on the RA8875 has not been observed** — it is `NYV`, and it is debug/dev only, so it is low priority but should not be silently assumed fine.
**What:** state the encoding in `data_sender.h` as part of the v2 string contract (UTF-8, receiver must tolerate a truncated trailing sequence), and decide deliberately whether the firmware should keep authoring non-ASCII at all. **Both options are defensible and the choice is the operator's:** keep UTF-8 and make the contract explicit, or restrict transmitted strings to ASCII so every consumer including a 7-bit font is safe. **No firmware edit is made here** — the board runs `26f2bee` and the only current source drift from it is a comment, which is worth preserving until there is a reason to reflash.
**Proof:** the encoding is stated on the wire contract; a truncation test exists on the receiver (it does — `test_a_truncated_multibyte_sequence_degrades_instead_of_raising`); and if ASCII-only is chosen, a guard that fails the build or a test when a transmitted literal contains a non-ASCII byte.

### TODO-62 — `capture.sh`'s low-rate warning is state-unaware and cried wolf on a good capture 🟡 **tooling wording, found 2026-09-27**
**What (`OBS`):** `capture.sh` compares the measured rate against the ~36 500 B/s **idle** figure and prints *"A byte count far below that is a link fault — fix it here before replaying anything."* The 1 kHz RESULT capture averaged **2 091 B/s** and triggered it. The capture was perfectly good: after a measurement completes, `presentation_updateLive()` is gated to `IDLE|ARMED`, so LIVE_FRAME production **stops by design** and only the 15/37/47-byte periodic packets remain. The same applies to any capture spanning ABORTED or RESULT.
**Why it matters:** the warning is otherwise valuable — it caught the `/dev/serial0` device fault on 2026-09-26 — so the fix is a caveat, **not** a relaxation. An unqualified "link fault" on a healthy artifact trains the operator to ignore the one message that has already prevented a false diagnosis, and it nearly produced the opposite error here: a complete, CRC-valid RESULT was briefly at risk of being written off as a link failure.
**What:** keep the threshold and add the state caveat — roughly *"~36.5 KB/s is the IDLE rate. Live frames stop during ABORTED, PROCESSING and RESULT, so a capture spanning a measurement is EXPECTED to be far below it. Replay before concluding anything: the replay report, not this rate line, is authoritative."* Consider printing the idle-equivalent seconds rather than only the rate.
**Proof:** the RESULT capture's log text no longer reads as a failure, and the zero-byte and short-capture paths are unchanged (their five regression tests still pass).

### TODO-58 — `ROUTER_STATUS` (0x05) is built, documented and never sent — and its schema transmits fabricated physical state 🟡 **SCHEMA CORRECTED AND LIFECYCLE IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-26. Compile-verified `_PI` and `_NONE`, NOT FLASHED, NOT BENCH-VERIFIED**
> **🔺 THE DESIGN PASS CHANGED WHAT THIS ITEM IS.** It began as "wire up the missing caller." It was not: the packet as designed would have transmitted **fabricated physical state**, so adding the call without correcting the schema would have made the defect worse, not better.
> **The fabrication (`OBS`):** all four jack-detect inputs are `INPUT_PULLUP` ([signal_router.cpp:208-211](../signal_router.cpp#L208)) with **nothing attached**. `config.h` documents three as *"not built, sense unverified"* in its own comments, and the fourth — instrument — is a **two-terminal jack with no switch terminal** (TODO-44), with *"pin 16 currently feeds nothing at all"* (TODO-5). The D2F-01L microswitches intended to provide detection are **parts-blocked, assume on hand = 0**. So every `jacks` bit was a pullup artefact, and `can_inject` inherited it.
> **✅ Corrected schema — frozen before first transmission.** Seven fixed bytes, unchanged in size: `jack_present`, **`jack_detect_available`** (new), `return_source`, `injection_active`, `monitor_mode`, reserved, `description_length`, then the description.
> **🔑 The receiver invariant, which is the whole point:** `jack_present` **must not** be read independently of `jack_detect_available`. Per detector — `available=0` → **UNKNOWN**; `available=1, present=0` → ABSENT; `available=1, present=1` → PRESENT. **A Boolean cannot carry "we have no detector"**, which is why two masks exist rather than one overloaded flag. All four availability bits are **0** today, so UNKNOWN is the expected answer, not an error state.
> **✅ `route_valid` and `can_inject` REMOVED from the wire.** `route_valid` was `(selected_return != NONE)` — a tautology under ADR-004, fully recomputable from `return_source`, with a name asserting physical connectivity it never established. `can_inject` was that tautology **AND** an unavailable detector, so its honest value is UNKNOWN and a Boolean cannot carry it. **Neither had any consumer:** `signalRouter_canInject()` and `signalRouter_canMeasure()` are defined and called nowhere, and [.ino:386](../guitar_rig_analyzer.ino#L386) records that the measurement engine does not consult them. They remain on `RouterStatus` for bench diagnostics; they are simply not transmitted, and **the Pi must not reconstruct either.**
> **✅ `injection_active` KEPT** — a primitive Teensy-owned fact answering *"is injection active right now?"*, which is a different question from HELLO's capability bit (*"can this build inject?"*). Constantly 0 today, and that is **truthful**, not a placeholder.
> **✅ Masking happens at the presentation boundary, not in the router.** `RouterStatus` keeps **raw** detector readings so bring-up diagnostics still see real pin state; `dataSender_sendRouterStatus()` ANDs presence with availability before transmitting. This is a **wire-contract obligation, not sanitising bad router data** — the raw GPIO value is not wrong, it simply is not evidence of presence when no detect hardware exists.
> **✅ Lifecycle: on-change + 1 Hz periodic repair, orchestrated in the `.ino`.** Immediate emission after a successful `signalRouter_setReturnSource()` and on `JACK_CHANGED`; the periodic path is **independently sufficient**, so a Pi attaching at an arbitrary moment converges within one second even if every on-change emission were lost. **No `signal_router` → `data_sender` coupling was introduced** — the application layer orchestrates, exactly as it already does for `acknowledgeAbort()` → `presentation_restoreLive()`. **1 Hz, not STATE's 500 ms:** route state is slowly-changing configuration, so it belongs in HELLO's cadence tier. *(The `JACK_CHANGED` branch is unreachable on current hardware — no detector can change — and is wired now so the lifecycle is complete and testable when detectors are fitted.)*
> **📌 Recorded deliberately: this is a v2 PRE-FIRST-TRANSMISSION correction.** The superseded layout had **zero firmware callers** and therefore never formed part of an observed or deployed wire contract. Correcting it in place costs no compatibility, and this was the cheapest moment it would ever be fixable. **`PI_PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 2.**
> **✅ ADR-004 preserved explicitly.** `return_source` is the sole authority on the selected return. The Pi must never infer *"XLR plug detected therefore XLR is active"* — Decision 3 forbids it, and today it would be inferred from a pullup. A test asserts an XLR plug present while TS is selected renders as exactly that.
> **✅ This packet is no longer the weakly-protected exception.** It previously had only a round-trip vector, which cannot catch a field reorder because both sides move together. It now has a **structural drift guard that reads the firmware builder**: every byte offset, the presence-AND-availability masking, the jack bit positions in both masks, and the absence of the removed fields. Plus a guard asserting all four availability flags are 0 — **if someone raises one, that test should fail, and the fix is to record the as-built evidence, not edit the test.**
> **🔺 EVIDENCE — compile and offline only.** `_PI` and `_NONE` compile clean with **zero warnings**. 164 Pi tests pass (138 → 164). Negative controls: dropping the availability mask from `payload[0]` fails the layout guard; raising a detector flag without evidence fails the availability guard. **Offline tests establish neither wire agreement nor physical jack detection. NOT FLASHED.**
**Remaining to close:** flash with the TODO-9 intermediate build; confirm on hardware that a Pi attached at an arbitrary moment obtains the current route within ~1 s without a jack change, that a `j`/`k` selection appears immediately, and that every jack renders UNKNOWN rather than ABSENT. **Physical jack detection remains unbuilt and is not in this item's scope** — TODO-44 and the D2F-01L parts gate own that.

<details><summary><b>Original finding, retained for the reasoning trail (2026-09-26)</b></summary>

**What (`OBS`):** `presentation_routerStatus()` is defined in **all three** backends ([presentation.cpp:68/130/179](../presentation.cpp#L68)) and `dataSender_sendRouterStatus()` builds a full payload ([data_sender.cpp:723](../data_sender.cpp#L723)) — **and nothing anywhere in the firmware calls either.** A whole-tree search returns only the definitions and the header declarations. The packet type, the payload builder, the wire documentation and the presentation API all exist; the call site does not.
**Why it matters:** the Pi therefore cannot learn **jack presence, return source, route validity, or monitor mode** — all of which the production Live view is specified to show ("active source/return routing"). It is the same failure shape the protocol audit named for the v1 findings: *the firmware computes something and does not put it on the wire* — except here the transmitter was written and simply never wired up.
**Note this is NOT a protocol defect.** Protocol v2 carries the packet correctly and the Pi parser decodes it (`packets.decode_router_status`, tested). The gap is entirely a missing producer call.
**What:** call `presentation_routerStatus()` where route state actually changes — `signalRouter_update()`'s `JACK_CHANGED`, `signalRouter_setReturnSource()`'s transition, and once after `signalRouter_init()` so a receiver has an initial snapshot. **Consider the TODO-56 lesson before choosing "on change only":** a Pi that attaches after the last change would never receive one. Whatever TODO-57 decides about periodic state announcement probably governs this too — **resolve TODO-57 first and reuse its mechanism rather than inventing a second cadence.**
**Proof:** a Pi attached at an arbitrary moment displays the current route and jack state, and a route change is reflected without a reconnect.

### TODO-39 — T1 acceptance harness (`timing_probe`) 🔴 **REOPENED 2026-09-29. Its one absolute pass/fail metric counts the wrong counter stride. Was 🟢 on compile proof alone; the first hardware execution broke it**
> **🔴 THE DEFECT, from T1 Run 2 (the harness's FIRST EVER runtime execution).** Run 2 reported `blocks_skipped=44490`, `skip_events=2966`, `largest_block_gap=16` — and `block_n` was also **2966**, i.e. *every single analysis block* was flagged as having skipped exactly 15 predecessors. The figure is pure arithmetic: **2966 × (16 − 1) = 44 490**, exactly, nothing left over.
> **The identity that settles it:** `block_ms_mean / largest_block_gap = 46.440 / 16 = 2.9025 ms`, and `128 samples / 44 100 Hz = 2.9025 ms`. Exact to four decimals.
> **Root cause (`OBS`, three source facts):** [timing_probe.cpp:89](../timing_probe.cpp#L89) tests `gap > 1` on the stated assumption that the counter advances once per analysis block. But [audio_engine.cpp:338](../audio_engine.cpp#L338) assigns `frame->output_block_number = block.audio_block_counter`, and [audio_capture.h:103](../audio_capture.h#L103) documents `audio_block_counter` as *"the master timebase. Increments once per"* **128-sample audio block**. An analysis block advances 2 048 samples = **16** audio blocks. **The true stride is 16; the detector expects 1.**
> **✅ Acquisition was HEALTHY — corroborated three independent ways.** The 5 s diagnostic block, which uses a different counter entirely, read **107–108 blocks per 5 000 ms** in *every* block across 137.8 s (it would read ~7 if 15 of 16 were really lost); the measured period was **46.440 ms with 0.089 ms spread over 2 966 blocks** (a real overrun would stretch the interval to ~743 ms); and **zero** packets of any class dropped. **Do not record 44 490 dropped blocks as a fact about the instrument.**
> **🛑 CONSEQUENCE: T1 CANNOT BE ADJUDICATED WITH THIS HARNESS.** `blocks_skipped` / `skip_events` / `largest_block_gap` are the only criteria the project states *absolutely* ("0 in every run, no exceptions" — runbook step 11, TODO-35), and all three are unevaluable. **The stride is a property of the firmware's own timebase, not of whether a Pi is attached, so this would have failed run 1 — the `_NONE` control — identically.** The matrix could never have passed as instrumented.
> **🔑 The project already knew the tick rate, on the other side of the link.** [`pi/analyzer_link/packets.py:194`](../pi/analyzer_link/packets.py#L194) documents the transmitted field as *"the lower 16 bits of the **audio block counter**"*, and [`pi/tools/replay.py:110`](../pi/tools/replay.py#L110) records **2.9027 ms/tick measured** against the 30 s hardware fixture versus 2.9025 theoretical. **The Pi-side decoder has been right all along; the firmware's own skip detector was wrong.** The two halves were never cross-checked, and `timing_probe` being compile-only is exactly why.
>
> **🔬 EVERY CONSUMER OF `output_block_number` INSPECTED BEFORE PROPOSING A FIX (asked for explicitly, 2026-09-29):**
> | Consumer | Uses it as | Effect of changing what is assigned |
> |---|---|---|
> | [timing_probe.cpp:89](../timing_probe.cpp#L89) | analysis-block sequence, stride 1 | **the only wrong consumer** |
> | [data_sender.cpp:627](../data_sender.cpp#L627) | transmits **lower 16 bits** on the wire, LIVE_FRAME bytes [2-3] | **wire contract change** |
> | [`packets.py:194`](../pi/analyzer_link/packets.py#L194) | decodes it, documented as the audio block counter | **correct today; would BREAK** |
> | [`state.py:57`](../pi/analyzer_link/state.py#L57) | link-staleness detection | would change meaning |
> | [audio_engine.h:195](../audio_engine.h#L195) | comment: *"Increments each time capture() returns true"* | **FALSE for the value assigned** |
> | `s_output_block_number` ([audio_capture.cpp:226](../audio_capture.cpp#L226)) | incremented at :791, reset at :509, **never read anywhere** | dead code; its comment at :224 claiming it *"appears in AudioRawBlock.output_block_number"* is **also false** |
>
> **➡️ THEREFORE THE FIX IS IN THE DETECTOR, NOT ON THE WIRE — and this is now evidence-backed, not a preference.** Changing the transmitted counter to make the test pass would break a Pi-side interpretation that is **already correct and already documented**, and would silently redefine LIVE_FRAME bytes [2-3] under an unchanged `PI_PROTOCOL_VERSION`. **Do not do it.** Fix `timingProbe_noteBlock()` to expect the real stride, derived from existing constants rather than a hard-coded 16, and flag `gap != expected` — **both a larger and a smaller gap are findings**, which the current `gap > 1` cannot express.
> **Fix the two false comments in the same pass** ([audio_engine.h:195](../audio_engine.h#L195) and [audio_capture.cpp:224](../audio_capture.cpp#L224)), and either delete dead `s_output_block_number` or give it a reader. A wrong comment fed a wrong instrument; leaving it is how this recurs.
> **🛑 NOT FIXED TONIGHT, deliberately.** The board runs `26f2bee`, which is the artifact all the 2026-09-27 RESULT-transport evidence was gathered against. Repairing instrumentation mid-matrix would invalidate it and force the Phase 10 evidence chain to be re-established. **Batch this with TODO-61/63/64 as one reviewed firmware change.**
> **Also owed: a re-run of Run 2.** Its window was **137.785 s** rather than the fixed 120 s (agent tool-call latency), which already exceeds the 10 % window tolerance `t1_diff.py` applies — so it could not have served as a window-matched matrix member even with a working skip metric.
> Records: [`t1-run2-pi-normal.txt`](evidence/t1-run2-pi-normal.txt), [`2026-09-29-t1-harness-block-stride-defect.md`](evidence/2026-09-29-t1-harness-block-stride-defect.md).

**Original 2026-09-11 entry, retained — note it claimed DONE on compile proof, and that is precisely the failure mode this project's own status legend warns about:**
### TODO-39 — T1 acceptance harness (`timing_probe`) — original status 🟢 **compile-verified 2026-09-11, all three targets**
**Why:** the T1 pass condition is "acquisition timing indistinguishable from the
`_NONE` control", and **the firmware could not produce the numbers that claim
requires.** The 5 s window reported a block-period *mean* only — no max, no
spread, no skipped-block detection, no audio high-water, no loop-body max. Phase 10
could have been "passed" on a small transport figure while a real perturbation went
unmeasured, which is precisely the failure mode the pass condition is written to
prevent.
**What was done:** `timing_probe.{h,cpp}` — block period mean/max/min/spread;
**skipped-block detection from gaps in the canonical block counter** (a gap > 1
means an analysis block was produced that the loop never processed — the most
direct evidence of measurement disturbance available without touching the capture
layer); whole-loop-body duration max, which catches anything blocking wherever it
lives rather than only presentation calls; audio block high-water with a per-window
reset so a boot spike cannot contaminate later runs. Wired into `loop()` and
`setup()`; `t` / `T` serial commands; emits the transport block in the same report
so one paste captures both sides.
**Compiled into all three targets deliberately** — guarding it to `_PI` would make
the control run unmeasurable and void the entire test.
**Honest limitation, stated in the source rather than glossed:** `audio_capture`
exposes no dropped-block or per-queue overrun counter, so `audio_blocks_high_water`
is the Audio library's **global** figure and is corroborating pressure data only.
`blocks_skipped` is the authoritative overrun evidence. The report says so, so
nobody cites it as "capture overruns".
**Proof:** all three targets compile clean (`_PI` and `_NONE` warning-free).
**Not yet uploaded** — compile proof only. `[compiles]`

### TODO-38 — Single authoritative firmware version; 0.6.0 baseline 🟢 **DONE 2026-09-11**
**Why:** `FIRMWARE_VERSION` said `0.5.0` while `data_sender.{h,cpp}` headers said `v0.6.1` and **21 other module headers carried their own `v0.5.0`**. A version stamped into every exported measurement must have exactly one source, and a module-local product version is drift waiting to be cited as fact.
**What was done:**
- `FIRMWARE_VERSION "0.6.0"` — a deliberate baseline, not a patch bump, and deliberately **not** 0.6.1 just because a header already claimed it (that header had run ahead of the product). 0.6.0 means one thing: *first firmware baseline implementing the Pi 5 production display architecture* — presentation boundary, three compile targets, backend exclusion, validation harness migrated off the renderer, protocol v2 with the four truth-invariant defects closed.
- `PI_PROTOCOL_VERSION 2` added as a **separate** constant. Firmware version and wire-format version are different compatibility concepts: a firmware bump that does not touch the wire must not invalidate a working receiver, and a wire change must be visible even if the firmware version is untouched. v1 → v2 because v2 changes the payload shape of both LIVE_FRAME and RESULT, and a version number that has appeared in the tree is not reused for a different layout.
- **Module-local product versions stripped from all 21 files** (headers and .cpp alike). Original file permissions preserved exactly — several were read-only and were restored to read-only afterward.
**Proof:** `grep -rn "Guitar Rig Analyzer[ ]*v0\.[0-9]" *.h *.cpp *.ino` returns nothing; all three targets compile; the banner and HELLO both report 0.6.0 from the single constant.

## Track B — Firmware-only (deferred until hardware catches up, or independent of it)

### TODO-11 — Complete the signal_router promotion (steps 3-6), one step per matching hardware phase — not ahead of it ⚪ blocked on Track A
**🚦 ADDED 2026-09-16 — PREREQUISITE before any internal-stimulus arm path becomes reachable (from the ADR-004 ordering audit, journal 2026-09-16 (5); code-derived).** `measurementEngine_update()` in **ARMED with internal stimulus does not consume the fresh MEASURE block** (`measurement_engine.cpp:784`), so a stale block can reach CAPTURE and open the raw-sample drain gate (`:831–835`, `:895`). Combined with `audioCap_update()` returning clip events before ROUTE_CHANGED (`audio_capture.cpp:792–803`), a return-source change could then let post-switch raw samples be accumulated, and potentially finish a mixed-topology RESULT, before the delayed abort, **if `abort_on_clip = false`**. Unreachable today (the only arm caller is `m`: EXTERNAL, `abort_on_clip = true`). **Close it before enabling injection. Do not pick the fix yet:** candidates include always consuming the block in ARMED and giving ROUTE_CHANGED priority over clip. Decide with its own trace once injection is active.
**🚦 ADDED 2026-09-13 — resolve the functional requirement behind Rig Send (pin 17) detection separately. See [ADR-003](../docs/adr/ADR-003-jack-presence-is-a-ux-feature.md).** Pin 17 gates `can_inject`, and `canInject()` is currently never called. **Do not assume a mechanical jack switch is the answer.** First define what is being prevented: if the concern is injecting a sweep with no rig connected, note that **a plug being present does not prove the downstream rig is connected correctly** — so plug presence may be the wrong signal entirely, not merely an inconvenient one. **Decide the requirement before selecting a mechanism.**
**Why:** `signal_router.h/.cpp`, `signal_router_config_additions.h`, and `audiocap_injection_api.h` are staging code from the documented promotion checklist (`development/README.md`), already moved to the repo root (step 2 done, no `development/` folder remains) but not integrated: no `s_mixer_send` in `audio_capture.cpp`, the generator still only feeds the monitor mixer, `measurement_engine.cpp` never calls `signalRouter_canInject()`. Confirmed by reading the actual files directly, twice (Mac tree and this Claude Project's own docs) — see journal.md 2026-08-25 (3) and (4). **Decision (2026-08-25 (4)):** do not move the files back into `development/` — they're already wired into the live Phase 2 smoke test (`RELAY_SEND_PIN`/`RELAY_RETURN_PIN`) and reverting would be pure source-layout churn with no hardware progress to show for it. Keep them at the repo root, but keep the promotion explicitly marked incomplete/in-progress — do not treat the TODO-1 compile fix as license to also merge the injection audio graph.
**What:** Complete checklist steps 3-6 incrementally, each gated on its own hardware phase actually existing and being testable, not speculatively ahead of it: merge the pin constants into `config.h` now (TODO-1 — pins are already correctly designed, this is integration only, not pin design); add `s_mixer_send` to `audio_capture.cpp` only once the relay/routing hardware it depends on (Phase 6/7) is on the bench; implement `audioCap_setInjectionMode` and the `measurement_engine` arm-time gate (`signalRouter_canInject()`) only once injection can be immediately tested against real hardware, not before.
**Proof:** an INTERNAL-stimulus MODE_LIVE run (not MODE_PRECISION — see TODO-13) shows the actual generated signal present in the RETURN_NODE capture with a rig connected — not silence, not today's monitor-only loopback.

**Why:** These modes exist conceptually in `measurement_core.h` but `arm()` rejects them (confirmed still true on 2026-08-25) because the OCRAM accumulator and bin-remapping logic don't exist yet.
**What:** Implement the OCRAM accumulator and frequency-bin remapping for MODE_PRECISION and MODE_LF; only remove the `arm()`-time rejection once the full path produces real, correctly-rebinned data end to end.

### TODO-14 — Touch UI interaction ⚪ **SUPERSEDED by ADR-001 — touch moves to the Pi**
**Status 2026-09-11:** touch interaction is a Pi-side deliverable (TODO-36), not an
RA8875 one. `RA8875_INT_PIN` stays defined for the debug backend but no touch
handler will be written for it. **Kept, not deleted** — the proof criterion below
is the valuable part and transfers verbatim: whatever drives a state transition,
touch or packet, must produce the *identical* transition the serial command
produces, and live updates must still only occur in IDLE / ARMED. Under ADR-001
this becomes a stronger constraint, since the Pi must route control actions back
through the same `measurement_engine` entry points rather than modelling state
locally.
**Why (original, retained):** `display_engine` renders the result view and insight panel; all measurement control still goes through the serial `x` command.
**What:** Add touch handling wired to the same `measurement_engine` control entry points the serial `x` command uses today.

### TODO-15 — Pink noise 1/f shaping 🔴 low priority
**Why:** `StimulusType::INTERNAL_PINK` exists as an enum value but `signal_generator.cpp` only implements white noise; the 1/f IIR shaping is explicitly stubbed. Could otherwise be mistaken for a real pink-noise source.
**What:** Implement the 1/f shaping filter in `signal_generator.cpp`.
**Proof:** measured spectrum of INTERNAL_PINK output shows a ~-3 dB/octave slope, not a flat (white) spectrum.

### TODO-16 — Real-hardware tolerance/threshold tuning (validation.cpp + insights_engine.cpp) 🔴 low priority — do after Track A produces consistent good measurements
**Why:** The validation harness and insight thresholds are deliberately conservative placeholders. **Don't "fix" wide tolerances before real characterisation — they're intentional** — but don't forget them either: a criterion that passes with large headroom gives weak assurance, and that remains the real problem this item exists to solve.
**🔺 MECHANISM REVERSED 2026-09-22 (`cec814a`, journal (4)) — the goal is unchanged, the method is not.** This item used to cite `validation.cpp`'s tighten-tolerance advisory as its trigger. That advisory is **removed**: it derived a new acceptance boundary from the same `ValidationResult` that had just been judged against the old one, and it could fire after a single run. *(Its trigger was also described here as ">2x headroom"; the code actually fired at measured < 50 % of tolerance.)* **The intent was reasonable and is preserved; only the post-hoc mechanism is rejected.**
**What:** Once sufficient hardware characterisation evidence exists, revise the provisional tolerance/threshold constants — **as a separate, justified design decision, journalled and frozen BEFORE the next acceptance run judges anything against the new values.** The evidence must come from characterisation runs, **never from the acceptance run the criterion will judge** (invariant recorded above the constants in `validation.h`). A revision may tighten or widen a constant; "tighten" is not the goal, **matching the criterion to demonstrated capability** is.
**Proof:** for each revised constant — the characterisation evidence and the new value are journalled **before** the run that uses them; the revision states what changed and why; and the next acceptance run is judged against the frozen new value. **A run whose criterion was adjusted after seeing its result is not evidence.** **⚠️ Not closed by `cec814a`:** Group D removed the bad mechanism from the firmware; it did **not** perform the characterisation this item exists for.

### TODO-17 — Sample-accurate (interrupt-level) generator alignment ⚪ only if accuracy requirements justify it
**Why:** Current alignment is block-level (~2.9 ms, one Audio Library block) by design; true interrupt-level alignment is explicitly future work in `signal_generator.cpp`. Only worth doing if a future latency/phase-accuracy claim needs tighter-than-one-block precision.
**What:** Patch the Audio Library interrupt handler for sample-accurate stimulus start.
**Proof:** measured start-alignment jitter is sub-sample, vs. today's ≤1-block bound.

## Completed (verified — do not re-report as bugs; see journal.md for the reasoning trail)

The following were already fixed as of the 2026-08-25 baseline and confirmed via the project's iterative bug-fix passes. Listed here so future sessions don't waste time re-discovering or re-litigating them:

- CRC-32 seed/final-XOR bug in calibration_store — 🟢
- `getRawSamples()` all-or-none contract — 🟢
- JSON escaping / non-finite (NaN/Inf → `null`) export handling in export_engine — 🟢
- Phase unwrap made segment-aware across NaN gaps — 🟢
- `capture_end_block` stamps the last accepted capture frame, not later processing time — 🟢
- TF erase logic respects previous validity gaps — 🟢
- Insight-band mean helpers return NaN on no valid bins; detectors guard against non-finite results — 🟢
- Validation preflight aborts on ROUTE_CHANGED — 🟢
- Rig-analysis factory uses EXTERNAL stimulus — 🟢
- Self-calibration factory uses EXTERNAL stimulus (DUT injection wiring itself tracked separately — see TODO-11) — 🟢
- Non-H1 estimators rejected instead of silently mislabeled — 🟢
- Route-change abort distinct from user cancel via `measurementEngine_notifyRouteChanged()` — 🟢
- Result screen wiring implemented; `x` from RESULT uses `measurementEngine_returnToLive()` — 🟢
